&=\frac{1}{N}\,\mathrm{tr}_{Z_{1}^{m}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{m+2}^{n}}\left[{\left({M_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{m}}^{z_{1}^{m}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{C_{B_{L}}\left({B_{R}}\right)_{m+1}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{m+2}^{n}}}\right)\Upsilon_{\left({B_{L}}\right)_{m+1}\rightarrow C_{B_{L}}}\circ\mathrm{tr}_{\left({\bar{B}}\right)_{m+1}}\left[{\rho_{\left(B\bar{B}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right]}\right]\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\autoref{eqn:SDP_constraints_appendix}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{N}\,\mathrm{tr}_{Z_{1}^{m}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{m+2}^{n}}\left[{\left({M_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{m}}^{z_{1}^{m}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{C_{B_{L}}\left({B_{R}}\right)_{m+1}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{m+2}^{n}}}\right)\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{m}}\otimes K_{C_{B_{L}}}\otimes\rho_{\left({B_{R}}\right)_{m+1}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{m+2}^{n}}}\right]\\
&=K_{C_{B_{L}}}\otimes\rho_{\left({B_{R}}\right)_{m+1}|z_{1}^{m}}\,.\end{split}

Lastly, from
Prop. A.4
,
\Xi_{B_{m+1}\rightarrow B_{m+1}}\circ\mathrm{tr}_{\bar{B}_{m+1}}\left({\cdot}\right)
acts as
\Xi_{B_{n}\rightarrow B_{n}}\circ\mathrm{tr}_{\bar{B}_{n}}\left({\cdot}\right)
for any
m\in\left[{n}\right]
. Thus, due to the commutativity with
\mathcal{M}_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{m}}
, we obtain

(61)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Xi_{B_{m+1}\rightarrow B_{m+1}}&\circ\mathrm{tr}_{\bar{B}_{m+1}}\left({\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{m+1}|z_{1}^{m}}}\right)=\\
&=\frac{1}{N}\,\mathrm{tr}_{Z_{1}^{m}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{m+2}^{n}}\left[{\left({M_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{m}}^{z_{1}^{m}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{B_{m+1}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{m+2}^{n}}}\right)\Xi_{B_{m+1}\rightarrow B_{m+1}}\circ\mathrm{tr}_{\bar{B}_{m+1}}\left[{\rho_{\left(B\bar{B}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right]}\right]\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\autoref{eqn:SDP_constraints_appendix}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{N}\,\mathrm{tr}_{Z_{1}^{m}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{m+2}^{n}}\left[{\left({M_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{m}}^{z_{1}^{m}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{B_{m+1}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{m+2}^{n}}}\right)\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{m}B_{m+1}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{m+2}^{n}}}\right]=\rho_{B|z_{1}^{m}}\,.\end{split}

As the foregoing arguments are independent of the particular choice of
z_{1}^{m}
, they hold for all
z_{1}^{m}
.
∎

5.4.
The hierarchy

In analogy to
[
Ber+21
]
, we can formulate a Bose-symmetric SDP hierarchy which is working to approximate a constrained separability problem
cSEP(G)
(see
App. A
) from above.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
cSEP(G)
be as in
Eq. 12
. Then,

(62)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\displaystyle&SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)=\max_{\rho_{A\left(B\bar{B}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\mathrm{tr}\left[{G_{AB}\,\rho_{AB}}\right]\\
\text{s.t. }\quad&\rho_{A\left(B\bar{B}\right)_{1}^{n}}\succeq 0,\quad\mathrm{tr}\left[\rho_{A\left(B\bar{B}\right)_{1}^{n}}\right]=1,\quad\rho_{A\left(B\bar{B}\right)_{1}^{n}}\text{ is Bose-symmetric w.r.t. }A,\,\\
\\
&\begin{array}[]{ll}\Theta_{A_{L}\rightarrow C_{A_{L}}}\left({\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right)=W_{C_{A_{L}}}\otimes\rho_{A_{R}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}},&\Omega_{A\rightarrow A}\left({\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right)=\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}},\\
\Upsilon_{\left({B_{L}}\right)_{n}\rightarrow C_{B_{L}}}\circ\mathrm{tr}_{\bar{B}_{n}}\left({\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right)=K_{C_{B_{L}}}\otimes\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n-1}\left({B_{R}}\right)_{n}},&\Xi_{B_{n}\rightarrow B_{n}}\circ\mathrm{tr}_{\bar{B}_{n}}\left({\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right)=\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n-1}B_{n}},\\
\end{array}\end{split}

such that

(63)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&0\leq SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)-cSEP(G)\leq\min\left\{{f(A|B\bar{B}),f(B\bar{B}|\cdot)}\right\}\sqrt{4\ln(2)}\sqrt{\frac{\ln(d_{A})}{n}}\,.\\
\end{split}

Proof.

Let
\rho_{AB}=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\,\rho_{A}^{x}\otimes\rho_{B}^{x}
be a feasible point to
cSEP(G)
. Moreover, let
\ket{\Psi}_{B\bar{B}}:=\sum_{i=1}^{d_{B}}\ket{i}_{B}\otimes\ket{i}_{\bar{B}}
denote the (non-normalized) maximally entangled state across the
\left({B:\bar{B}}\right)
cut, where
\bar{B}
is a purification system for
B
. Construct

(64)

\displaystyle\rho_{A\left(B\bar{B}\right)_{1}^{n}}:=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\,\rho_{A}^{x}\otimes\left(\ket{\psi^{x}}\bra{\psi^{x}}_{B\bar{B}}\right)^{\otimes n},\quad\ket{\psi^{x}}_{B\bar{B}}:=\left(\sqrt{\rho_{B}^{x}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\bar{B}}\right)\ket{\Psi}_{B\bar{B}},\,

where, for each
x\in\mathcal{X}
,
\ket{\psi^{x}}_{B\bar{B}}
is the pretty-good purification. By construction,
\rho_{A\left(B\bar{B}\right)_{1}^{n}}
is a state. Note that
\sqrt{\left(\rho_{B}^{x}\right)^{\otimes n}}=\sqrt{\rho_{B}^{x}}^{\otimes n}
and
\left({U_{B_{1}^{n}}(\pi)\otimes U_{\bar{B}_{1}^{n}}(\pi)}\right)\left({\ket{\Psi}\bra{\Psi}_{B\bar{B}}}\right)^{\otimes n}=\left({\ket{\Psi}\bra{\Psi}_{B\bar{B}}}\right)^{\otimes n}.\,
Since

(65)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left({\mathcal{I}_{A}\otimes U_{B_{1}^{n}}(\pi)\otimes U_{\bar{B}_{1}^{n}}(\pi)}\right)\rho_{A\left(B\bar{B}\right)_{1}^{n}}\\
&=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p_{x}\rho_{A}^{x}\otimes\left[\left({U_{B_{1}^{n}}(\pi)\otimes U_{\bar{B}_{1}^{n}}(\pi)}\right)\left({\sqrt{\rho_{B}^{x}}^{\otimes n}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\bar{B}_{1}^{n}}}\right)\left({\ket{\Psi}\bra{\Psi}_{B\bar{B}}}\right)^{\otimes n}\left({\sqrt{\rho_{B}^{x}}^{\otimes n}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\bar{B}_{1}^{n}}}\right)^{*}\right]\\
&=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\rho_{A}^{x}\otimes\left[{\left({U_{B_{1}^{n}}(\pi)\sqrt{\rho_{B}^{x}}^{\otimes n}\otimes U_{\bar{B}_{1}^{n}}}\right)\left({\ket{\Psi}\bra{\Psi}_{B\bar{B}}}\right)^{\otimes n}\left({\sqrt{\rho_{B}^{x}}^{\otimes n}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\bar{B}_{1}^{n}}}\right)^{*}}\right]\\
&=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\rho_{A}^{x}\otimes\left[{\left({U_{B_{1}^{n}}(\pi)\sqrt{\rho_{B}^{x}}^{\otimes n}\otimes U_{\bar{B}_{1}^{n}}}\right)\left({U^{T}_{B_{1}^{n}}U_{B_{1}^{n}}\otimes U^{T}_{\bar{B}_{1}^{n}}U_{\bar{B}_{1}^{n}}}\right)\left({\ket{\Psi}\bra{\Psi}_{B\bar{B}}}\right)^{\otimes n}\left({\sqrt{\rho_{B}^{x}}^{\otimes n}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\bar{B}_{1}^{n}}}\right)^{*}}\right]\\
&=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\rho_{A}^{x}\otimes\left[{\left({U_{B_{1}^{n}}(\pi)\sqrt{\rho_{B}^{x}}^{\otimes n}U^{T}_{B_{1}^{n}}\otimes U_{\bar{B}_{1}^{n}}U^{T}_{\bar{B}_{1}^{n}}}\right)\left({U_{B_{1}^{n}}\otimes U_{\bar{B}_{1}^{n}}}\right)\left({\ket{\Psi}\bra{\Psi}_{B\bar{B}}}\right)^{\otimes n}\left({\sqrt{\rho_{B}^{x}}^{\otimes n}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\bar{B}_{1}^{n}}}\right)^{*}}\right]\\
&=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\rho_{A}^{x}\otimes\left[{\left({\underbrace{U_{B_{1}^{n}}(\pi)\sqrt{\rho_{B}^{x}}^{\otimes n}U^{T}_{B_{1}^{n}}}_{=\sqrt{\rho_{B}^{x}}^{\otimes n}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\bar{B}_{1}^{n}}}\right)\underbrace{\left({U_{B_{1}^{n}}\otimes U_{\bar{B}_{1}^{n}}}\right)\left({\ket{\Psi}\bra{\Psi}_{B\bar{B}}}\right)^{\otimes n}}_{=\left({\ket{\Psi}\bra{\Psi}_{B\bar{B}}}\right)^{\otimes n}}\left({\sqrt{\rho_{B}^{x}}^{\otimes n}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\bar{B}_{1}^{n}}}\right)^{*}}\right]\\
&=\rho_{A\left(B\bar{B}\right)_{1}^{n}},\,\end{split}

the candidate is Bose-symmetric w.r.t.
A
. Moreover, since
\rho_{AB}
is feasible in
cSEP(G)
,

(66)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Theta_{A_{L}\rightarrow C_{A_{L}}}\left({\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right)&=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\,\Theta_{A_{L}\rightarrow C_{A_{L}}}\left({\rho_{A}^{x}}\right)\otimes\left(\ket{\psi^{x}}\bra{\psi^{x}}_{B\bar{B}}\right)^{\otimes n}\\
&=W_{C_{A_{L}}}\otimes\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\rho^{x}_{A_{R}}\,\otimes\left(\ket{\psi^{x}}\bra{\psi^{x}}_{B\bar{B}}\right)^{\otimes n}=W_{C_{A_{L}}}\otimes\rho_{A_{R}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}\end{split}

and

(67)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Omega_{A\rightarrow A}\left({\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right)&=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\,\Omega_{A\rightarrow A}\left({\rho_{A}^{x}}\right)\otimes\left(\ket{\psi^{x}}\bra{\psi^{x}}_{B\bar{B}}\right)^{\otimes n}\\
&=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\,\rho_{A}^{x}\otimes\left(\ket{\psi^{x}}\bra{\psi^{x}}_{B\bar{B}}\right)^{\otimes n}=\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}.\,\end{split}

Moreover, by symmetry of the constructed state, the linear maps in
cSEP(G)
act identically on each copy system. Thus,

(68)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Upsilon_{\left({B_{L}}\right)_{n}\rightarrow C_{B_{L}}}\circ\mathrm{tr}_{\bar{B}_{n}}\left({\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right)&=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\ket{\psi^{x}}\bra{\psi^{x}}_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n-1}}\otimes\Upsilon_{\left({B_{L}}\right)_{n}\rightarrow C_{B_{L}}}\circ\mathrm{tr}_{\bar{B}_{n}}\left({\ket{\psi^{x}}\bra{\psi^{x}}_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{n}}}\right)\\
&=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\ket{\psi^{x}}\bra{\psi^{x}}_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n-1}}\otimes\Upsilon_{\left({B_{L}}\right)_{n}\rightarrow C_{B_{L}}}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\circ\underbrace{\mathrm{tr}_{\bar{B}_{n}}\left({\left({\sqrt{\rho_{B}^{x}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\bar{B}}}\right)\ket{\Psi}\bra{\Psi}_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{n}}\left({\sqrt{\rho_{B}^{x}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\bar{B}}}\right)^{*}}\right)}_{=\rho^{x}_{B_{n}}}\\
&=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\ket{\psi^{x}}\bra{\psi^{x}}_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n-1}}\otimes\Upsilon_{\left({B_{L}}\right)_{n}\rightarrow C_{B_{L}}}\left({\rho^{x}_{B_{n}}}\right)\\
&=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\ket{\psi^{x}}\bra{\psi^{x}}_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n-1}}\otimes K_{C_{B_{L}}}\otimes\rho^{x}_{\left({B_{R}}\right)_{n}}=K_{C_{B_{L}}}\otimes\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n-1}\left({B_{R}}\right)_{n}},\,\end{split}

and

(69)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Xi_{B_{n}\rightarrow B_{n}}\circ\mathrm{tr}_{\bar{B}_{n}}\left({\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right)&=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\ket{\psi^{x}}\bra{\psi^{x}}_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n-1}}\otimes\Xi_{B_{n}\rightarrow B_{n}}\circ\mathrm{tr}_{\bar{B}_{n}}\left({\ket{\psi^{x}}\bra{\psi^{x}}_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{n}}}\right)\\
&=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\ket{\psi^{x}}\bra{\psi^{x}}_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n-1}}\otimes\Xi_{B_{n}\rightarrow B_{n}}\left({\rho^{x}_{B_{n}}}\right)=\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n-1}B_{n}}.\,\end{split}

By construction, we have
\mathrm{tr}_{\bar{B}_{1}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{2}^{n}}\left[{\rho_{A\left(B\bar{B}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right]=\rho_{AB}
, recovering the original state and certifying that any
\rho_{AB}
feasible in
cSEP(G)
, via the construction above, yields a feasible point in
SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)
with the same objective value. Thus, optimality is preserved. Conversely, the Bose-symmetric de Finetti theorem in
Lem. 5.7
certifies that any feasible point in
SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)
is close in
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{1}
-distance to a feasible state in
cSEP(G)
. Lastly, we prove the rate of convergence to
cSEP(G)
, or in other words, how well
SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)
approximates
cSEP(G)
, i.e.

(70)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\lvert SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)-cSEP(G)\right\rvert&\leq\left\lvert G_{AB}\left({\rho_{AB}-\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}p(x)\rho_{A}^{x}\otimes\rho_{B}^{x}}\right)\right\rvert\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{H\"{o}lder Ineq.}}}{{\leq}}\left\lVert G_{AB}\right\rVert_{\infty}\left\lVert\rho_{AB}-\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\rho_{A}^{x}\otimes\rho_{B}^{x}\right\rVert_{1}=\left\lVert\rho_{AB}-\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\rho_{A}^{x}\otimes\rho_{B}^{x}\right\rVert_{1}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Data processing Ineq.}}}{{\leq}}\left\lVert\rho_{A(B\bar{B})}-\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\rho_{A}^{x}\otimes\rho_{(B\bar{B})}^{x}\right\rVert_{1}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\autoref{lem:bose-symmetric_deFinetti}}}{{\leq}}\min\left\{{f(A|B\bar{B}),f(B\bar{B}|\cdot)}\right\}\sqrt{4\ln(2)}\sqrt{\frac{\ln(\left\lvert{A}\right\rvert)}{n}}.\,\end{split}

Note, that we have used the bound from the Bose-symmetric de Finetti theorem in
Lem. 5.7
as we obtained
\rho_{AB}
as an optimal point to
SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)
, i.e. from
\rho_{A(B\bar{B})_{1}^{n}}
.
∎

5.5.
SDP complexity

Our motivation for introducing the Bose-symmetric hierarchy stems from the ability to implicitly enforce the Bose-symmetric condition by restricting the optimization space in the SDP of
Lem. 5.8
to operators supported on and ranging over
A\otimes\lor^{n}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)
. In the constrained setting, this restriction is only valid if the chosen linear maps and fixed operators preserve this structure.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
\left\lvert{C_{A_{L}}}\right\rvert\leq\left\lvert{A_{L}}\right\rvert
and
\left\lvert{C_{B_{L}}}\right\rvert\leq\left\lvert{B_{L}}\right\rvert
. Then, the value of cSEP
cSEP(G)
from
Eq. 12
can be determined up to an additive error
\epsilon\geq 0

by
SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)
by going to levels

(71)

\displaystyle n\geq\mathcal{O}\left({\frac{\min\left\{{f(A|B\bar{B}),f(B\bar{B}|\cdot)}\right\}^{2}\log\left\lvert{A}\right\rvert}{\epsilon^{2}}}\right)

involving an optimization over
\mathcal{O}\left({\left\lvert{A}\right\rvert n^{\left\lvert{B}\right\rvert^{2}}}\right)
many variables.

Proof.

The linear map
\Theta_{A_{L}\rightarrow C_{A_{L}}}
acts locally on
A
, and thus, commutes with the
\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]
-action on
\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}
. Hence,
\Theta_{A_{L}\rightarrow C_{A_{L}}}\left({\rho_{A\left(B\bar{B}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right)
is symmetric w.r.t.
\mathrm{Im}\left({\Theta_{A_{L}\rightarrow C_{A_{L}}}}\right)\subseteq C_{A_{L}}
. The completely-positive and trace-preserving (cptp)-map
\mathrm{tr}_{A_{L}}\left[{\cdot}\right]
translates the symmetry of
\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}
w.r.t.
A
to a symmetry of
\rho_{A_{R}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}
w.r.t. to
A_{R}
. Moreover, since
W_{C_{A_{L}}}\in C_{A_{L}}

(72)

\displaystyle\Theta_{A_{L}\rightarrow C_{A_{L}}}\left({\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right),\,W_{C_{A_{L}}}\otimes\rho_{A_{R}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}\,\in\,\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({C_{A_{L}}\otimes A_{R}\otimes\vee^{n}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)}\right).\,

Note, however, for
\Theta_{A_{L}\rightarrow C_{A_{L}}}\left({\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right)
to be a valid quantum state,
\Theta_{A_{L}\rightarrow C_{A_{L}}}
needs to be a completely positive and trace preserving map, i.e.
W_{C_{A_{L}}}\succeq 0
and
\mathrm{tr}\left[{W_{C_{A_{L}}}}\right]=1
.

Moreover,
\mathrm{Im}\left({\Omega_{A\rightarrow A}}\right)\subseteq A
implies

(73)

\displaystyle\Omega_{A\rightarrow A}\left({\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right),\,\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}\,\in\,\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({A\otimes\vee^{n}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)}\right).

Since
S_{n-1}\subset S_{n}
,

(74)

\displaystyle\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}\in\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\lor^{n}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)}\right)\Rightarrow\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}\in\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n-1}}\right]}\left({\lor^{n-1}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)\otimes\left({B\bar{B}}\right)}\right),\,

or in other words

(75)

\displaystyle\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\lor^{n}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)}\right)\subset\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n-1}}\right]}\left({\lor^{n-1}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)\otimes\left({B\bar{B}}\right)}\right).\,

Since
\mathrm{tr}_{B_{n}}\left[{\cdot}\right]
is a cptp-map acting locally on
B_{n}
, with
Prop. A.4
, any
\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n-1}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{n}}\in\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n-1}}\right]}\left({\lor^{n-1}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)\otimes\left({B\bar{B}}\right)}\right)
gets mapped to
\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n-1}B_{n}}\in\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n-1}}\right]}\left({\lor^{n-1}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)\otimes B}\right)
. Thus, with
\mathrm{Im}\left({\Xi_{B_{n}\rightarrow B_{n}}}\right)\subseteq B_{n}
, we have

(76)

\displaystyle\Xi_{B_{n}\rightarrow B_{n}}\circ\mathrm{tr}_{\bar{B}_{n}}\left({\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right),\,\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n-1}B_{n}}\in\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\vee^{n-1}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)\otimes B_{n}}\right).\,

Furthermore, the linear map
\Upsilon_{\left({B_{L}}\right)_{n}\rightarrow C_{B_{L}}}
acts locally on
B_{n}
, thus, translating the symmetry of
\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n-1}B_{n}}
w.r.t.
B_{n}
to the symmetry of
\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n-1}\left({B_{L}}\right)_{n}}
w.r.t.
\left({B_{L}}\right)_{n}
. Since
\mathrm{Im}\left({\Upsilon_{\left({B_{L}}\right)_{n}\rightarrow C_{B_{L}}}}\right)\subseteq C_{B_{L}}
and
K_{C_{B_{L}}}\in C_{B_{L}}
,
Prop. A.4
implies

(77)

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{\left({B_{L}}\right)_{n}\rightarrow C_{B_{L}}}\circ\mathrm{tr}_{\bar{B}_{n}}\left({\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right),\,K_{C_{B_{L}}}\otimes\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n-1}\left({B_{R}}\right)_{n}}\,\in\,\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n-1}}\right]}\left({C_{B_{L}}\otimes\vee^{n-1}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)\otimes\left({B_{R}}\right)_{n}}\right)\,.

The arguments above allow us to restrict the optimization to those spaces. By
Prop. C.10
,

(78)

\displaystyle\text{dim}_{\mathbb{C}}\left({A\otimes\lor^{n}\left({B\otimes\bar{B}}\right)}\right)=\left\lvert{A}\right\rvert\cdot\binom{\left\lvert{B}\right\rvert\left\lvert{\bar{B}}\right\rvert+n-1}{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left\lvert{B}\right\rvert=\left\lvert{\bar{B}}\right\rvert}}{{\leq}}\left\lvert{A}\right\rvert\cdot(n+1)^{\left\lvert{B}\right\rvert^{2}},

and thus, the SDP’s at level
n
can be formulated in terms
\mathcal{O}\left({\left\lvert{A}\right\rvert n^{\left\lvert{B}\right\rvert^{2}}}\right),
i.e. polynomial in
n
many variables. The error of approximation follows directly from the Bose-symmetric de Finetti bound in
Lem. 5.7
.
∎

5.6.
Application to quantum non-local games

In the free non-local game setting, the value of the game can be upper bounded via the following Bose-symmetric SDP hierarchy:

(79)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\begin{array}[]{ll}&\displaystyle SDP_{n}^{Bose}\left({T,V,\pi}\right)=\left\lvert{T}\right\rvert\max_{\rho}\mathrm{tr}\left[{\left({V_{A_{1}A_{2}Q_{1}Q_{2}}\otimes S_{T\tilde{T}}}\right)\rho_{\left({A_{1}Q_{1}T}\right)\left({A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T}}\right)}}\right]\\
&\\
\text{s.t.}&\rho_{\left({A_{1}Q_{1}T}\right)\left({A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T}}\right)}=\mathrm{tr}_{\left({\left({A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T}}\right)\left({\overline{A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T}}}\right)}\right)_{2}^{n}}\left[{\rho_{(A_{1}Q_{1}T)\left((A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T})(\overline{A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T}})\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right]\\
&\\
&\rho_{(A_{1}Q_{1}T)\left((A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T})(\overline{A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T}})\right)_{1}^{n}}\text{ is Bose-symmetric state w.r.t.\ }\left({A_{1}Q_{1}T}\right)\\
&\mathrm{tr}_{A_{1}}\left[{\rho_{(A_{1}Q_{1}T)\left((A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T})(\overline{A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T}})\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right]=\sum_{q_{1}\in Q_{1}}\pi_{1}(q_{1})\ket{q_{1}}\bra{q_{1}}_{Q_{1}}\otimes\rho_{T\left((A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T})(\overline{A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T}})\right)_{1}^{n}}\\
&\\
&\mathrm{tr}_{\left({A_{2}}\right)_{n}(\overline{A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T}})_{n}}\left[{\rho_{\left((A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T})(\overline{A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T}})\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right]=\rho_{\left((A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T})(\overline{A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T}})\right)_{1}^{n-1}}\otimes\left({\sum_{q_{2}\in Q_{2}}\pi_{2}(q_{2})\ket{q_{2}}\bra{q_{2}}_{Q_{2}}\otimes\frac{\mathcal{I}_{\tilde{T}}}{\left\lvert{T}\right\rvert}}\right).\\
\end{array}\end{split}

The following result characterizes the computational complexity of the problem.

Lemma 5.10

(Restatement of
Lem. 2.2
)
.

For a given non-local free game with
\left\lvert{A}\right\rvert
answers,
\left\lvert{Q}\right\rvert
questions, a shared quantum state of dimension
\left\lvert{T}\right\rvert
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the optimal winning probability can be approximated up to an additive error
\epsilon\geq 0
by an SDP with

(80)

\displaystyle\exp\left[{\mathcal{O}\left({\left\lvert{AQT}\right\rvert\log\left\lvert{AQT}\right\rvert\log\left({\min\left\{{18^{3}\left\lvert{T}\right\rvert^{3},2\left\lvert{T}\right\rvert^{4}}\right\}}\right)\log\left({\frac{\log\left\lvert{AQT}\right\rvert}{\epsilon^{2}}}\right)}\right)}\right]\,

many variables.

Proof.

We apply the constrained Bose-symmetric de Finetti theorem
Lem. 5.9
with
A_{L}=A_{1}
,
A_{M}=Q_{1}
,
A_{R}=T
,
B_{L}=A_{2}
,
B_{R}=Q_{2}\hat{T}
,
X_{C_{A_{M}}}=\sum_{q_{1}\in Q_{1}}\pi_{1}(q_{1})\ket{q_{1}}\bra{q_{1}}_{Q_{1}}
and
Y_{C_{B_{R}}}=\sum_{q_{2}\in Q_{2}}\pi_{2}(q_{2})\ket{q_{2}}\bra{q_{2}}_{Q_{2}}
to obtain

(81)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\lVert\rho_{(A_{1}Q_{1}T)(A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T})}-\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p(x)\rho^{x}_{(A_{1}Q_{1}T)}\otimes\rho^{x}_{(A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T})}\right\rVert_{1}\\
&\leq\left\lVert\rho_{(A_{1}Q_{1}T)\left((A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T})(\overline{A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T}})\right)}-\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}p_{i}\rho^{x}_{(A_{1}Q_{1}T)}\otimes\rho^{x}_{(A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T})(\overline{A_{2}Q_{2}\tilde{T}})}\right\rVert_{1}\\
&\leq\min\left\{{\sqrt{18^{3}\left\lvert{T\tilde{T}\overline{\tilde{T}}}\right\rvert},2\left\lvert{\overline{\tilde{T}}\tilde{T}}\right\rvert}\right\}\sqrt{4\ln 2}\sqrt{\frac{\log\left\lvert{A_{1}Q_{1}T}\right\rvert}{n}}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left\lvert{T}\right\rvert=\left\lvert{\tilde{T}}\right\rvert=\left\lvert{\overline{\tilde{T}}}\right\rvert,\left\lvert{A_{1}}\right\rvert=\left\lvert{A}\right\rvert,\left\lvert{Q_{1}}\right\rvert=\left\lvert{Q}\right\rvert}}{{=}}\min\left\{{\sqrt{18^{3}\left\lvert{T}\right\rvert^{3}},2\left\lvert{T}\right\rvert^{2}}\right\}\sqrt{4\ln 2}\sqrt{\frac{\log\left\lvert{AQT}\right\rvert}{n}}.\,\end{split}

Recall that classical systems do not contribute to measurement distortion. Thus, we have

(82)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\lvert{SDP_{n}^{Bose}\left({T,V,\pi}\right)-w_{Q(T)}(V,\pi)}\right\rvert\leq\lvert T\rvert^{2}\min\left\{{\sqrt{18^{3}\left\lvert{T}\right\rvert^{3}},2\left\lvert{T}\right\rvert^{2}}\right\}\sqrt{4\ln 2}\sqrt{\frac{\log\left\lvert{AQT}\right\rvert}{n}},\,\end{split}

or equivalently, we can approximate
w_{Q(T)}(V,\pi)
up to an additive
\epsilon
-error with
SDP_{n}^{Bose}\left({T,V,\pi}\right)
when going to levels
n
of order

(83)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{O}\left({\min\left\{{18^{3}\left\lvert{T}\right\rvert^{3},2\left\lvert{T}\right\rvert^{4}}\right\}\frac{\lvert T\rvert^{4}\log\left\lvert{AQT}\right\rvert}{\epsilon^{2}}}\right).\,\end{split}

As in the proof of
Lem. 5.9
, we can restrict the optimization to the space of Bose-symmetric operators. Thus, with
Eq. 78
, in the SDP in
Eq. 79
we optimize over

(84)

\displaystyle\left\lvert{AQT}\right\rvert\left({\lvert T\rvert^{4}\min\left\{{18^{3}\left\lvert{T}\right\rvert^{3},2\left\lvert{T}\right\rvert^{4}}\right\}4\ln 2\frac{\log\left\lvert{AQT}\right\rvert}{\epsilon^{2}}+1}\right)^{\left\lvert{AQT}\right\rvert^{2}}

many variables.
∎

6.
Approximating quantum non-local games via symmetric subspace methods

6.1.
Introduction

Semidefinite programs with inherent symmetry arise in numerous areas of research, motivating substantial work on methods to reduce their complexity
[
Sch05
,
Lau06
,
GST06
,
DPS07
,
Pol20
]
. For an overview, see
[
Val09
]
. Related works have also examined the algorithmic complexity of these procedures in specialized cases where the symmetry is governed by the symmetric group, such as those involving the Terwilliger algebra
[
Gij09
]
. More recently,
[
FST22
,
CTV24
]
explored applications in approximate quantum error correction. Building on this line of research, we establish connections to concepts from
[
Chr+07
]
using Schur-Weyl duality.

The goal of this section is to develop an algorithm that expresses
SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)
in terms of the Schur basis. To clarify, while
SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)
is defined as an SDP restricted to operators with support and range in
A\otimes\vee^{n}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)
, it is not, a priori, expressed in a basis that yields a representation whose size scales only polynomially with
n
. To address this, in
Sec. 6.2
we introduce
\Psi\left({SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)}\right)
, a reformulation of
SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)
in a (Schur) basis, wherein the size of all objects is guaranteed, by construction, to grow only polynomially in
n
. In the
Sec. 6.3
, we demonstrate that the transformation from
SDP_{n}^{Bose}
to its Schur-basis reformulation
\Psi\left({SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)}\right)
can be performed efficiently in the parameter
n
. It is crucial to avoid first constructing
SDP_{n}^{Bose}
, followed by a basis transformation to obtain
\Psi\left({SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)}\right)
, as this would require handling exponentially large objects in
n
. Instead, we show in the how
\Psi\left({SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)}\right)
can be constructed directly, in a manner that is efficient with respect to
n
.

Notation

Let
\lambda=\left({\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{k}}\right)\in\mathbb{N}
or
\lambda\vdash_{k}n
be a partition or type of
n\in\mathbb{N}
into at most
k\in\mathbb{N}
parts and let
\text{Par}(k,n)
be the set of all such partitions. For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
we denote by
\left[{k}\right]
the set of natural numbers
1,\ldots,n
. For
\lambda\vdash_{k}n
a Young shape or diagram
Y(\lambda)
is an arrangement of
n
boxes into at most
k
rows. A semistandard tableau of
Y(\lambda)
is a labeling of the boxes which is strictly increasing within each column and non-decreasing within each row where the entries are from
\left[{k}\right]
and repetitions are allowed. For any partition
\lambda
, we denote the set of semistandard labelings of Young shape
Y(\lambda)
with entries from
\left[{k}\right]
by
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,\,k}
. Recording the number of times each number from
\left[{k}\right]
appears in the semistandard labeling of Young shape
Y(\lambda)
as a sequence or vector gives the weight or type of the tableau.

6.2.
A constrained Bose-symmetric SDP hierarchy of polynomial size

We state the following proposition which we derive in
Sec. C.1
.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
G
be the trivial group,
W_{1},W_{2}
two Hilbert spaces,
\tau
be a semistandard labeling with entries from
\left[{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\right]
of Young shape
Y((n))
and
\left\{{e_{i}}\right\}_{i=1}^{d_{\mathcal{H}}}
a canonical basis of
\mathcal{H}
. Consider the image of the corresponding Young symmetrizer in
\mathcal{H}^{\otimes n}
(polytabloid), i.e.

(85)

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{H}}^{n}}\ni u_{\tau}=\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\sim\tau}\bigotimes_{y\in Y((n))}e_{\tau^{\prime}(y)},\,

where
\tau^{\prime}\sim\tau
is any tableau which lies in the orbit of
\tau
under the row stabilizer subgroup of
\tau
. The map

(86)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\psi\,:\,\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{G}\right]\times\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]\times\mathbb{C}\left[{G}\right]}\left({W_{1}\otimes\vee^{n}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)\otimes W_{2}}\right)&\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{d_{W_{1}}\cdot d_{W_{2}}\cdot m_{(n)}\times d_{W_{1}}\cdot d_{W_{2}}\cdot m_{(n)}}\\
A&\mapsto\left({\mathcal{I}_{W_{1}}\otimes U_{(n)}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{W_{2}}}\right)^{T}A\left({\mathcal{I}_{W_{1}}\otimes U_{(n)}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{W_{1}}}\right)\end{split}

with
m_{(n)}=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\left({\vee^{n}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)}\right)
,
d_{W_{i}}=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\left({W_{i}}\right)
,
i=1,2
and with
U_{(n)}
containing the
u_{\tau}
from semistandard labelings of
Y((n))
as column vectors, is a bijection preserving positive semi-definiteness.

With our proposed isomorphism, we can formulate an optimization problem approximating
w_{Q(T)}(V,\pi)
from above, where the number of variables and number of constraints grow only polynomial in
n
. Given the cumbersome notation in this and the subsequent section, we frame the proofs in the context of more general systems. Let
\mathcal{H}_{A}=\mathcal{H}_{A_{L}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{A_{M}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{A_{R}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{B}=\mathcal{H}_{B_{L}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{B_{R}}
and let
X_{C_{A_{M}}}\in A_{M}
and
Y_{C_{B_{R}}}\in(B_{R})_{n}
. Since
\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}\in\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({A\otimes\vee^{n}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)}\right)
, we deduce

(87)

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{A_{L}}\left[{\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right],\,X_{C_{A_{M}}}\otimes\rho_{A_{R}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}\,\in\,\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({C_{A_{M}}\otimes A_{R}\otimes\vee^{n}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)}\right)

and

(88)

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{\left({B_{L}}\right)_{n}}\circ\mathrm{tr}_{\left({\bar{B}_{n}}\right)}\left[{\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right],\,\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n-1}}\otimes Y_{C_{B_{R}}}\,\in\,\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\vee^{n-1}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)\otimes(B_{R})_{n}}\right),\,

by analogous arguments as in the proof of
Lem. 5.9
. Thus,

(89)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\begin{array}[]{cc}&\displaystyle SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)=\max_{\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}\in\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({A\otimes\vee^{n}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)}\right)}\mathrm{tr}\left[G_{AB}\,\rho_{AB}\right]\\
\text{s.t.}&\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}\succeq 0,\,\mathrm{tr}\left[\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}\right]=1,\\
&\mathrm{tr}_{A_{L}}\left[{\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right]=X_{C_{A_{M}}}\otimes\rho_{A_{R}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}},\\
&\mathrm{tr}_{\left({B_{L}}\right)_{n}}\circ\mathrm{tr}_{\left({\bar{B}_{n}}\right)}\left[{\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}}\right]=\rho_{\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n-1}}\otimes Y_{C_{B_{R}}},\end{array}\end{split}

with
A_{L}=A_{1}
,
A_{M}=Q_{1}
,
A_{R}=T
,
B_{L}=A_{2}
,
B_{R}=Q_{2}\hat{T}
,
X_{C_{A_{M}}}=\sum_{q_{1}\in Q_{1}}\pi_{1}(q_{1})\ket{q_{1}}\bra{q_{1}}_{Q_{1}}
and
Y_{C_{B_{R}}}=\sum_{q_{2}\in Q_{2}}\pi_{2}(q_{2})\ket{q_{2}}\bra{q_{2}}_{Q_{2}}
is equivalent as an optimization problem to
SDP_{n}^{Bose}\left({T,V,\pi}\right)
in
Eq. 79
. For ease of notation, let

(90)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}:=\left\{{A\otimes\vee^{n}\left({B\bar{B}}\right),\,A_{M}\otimes A_{R}\otimes\vee^{n}\left({B\bar{B}}\right),\,\vee^{n-1}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)\otimes C_{B_{R}}}\right\}

and

(91)

\displaystyle\begin{split}m\,:\,\mathcal{D}&\rightarrow\mathbb{N}\\
\mathcal{D}_{i}&\mapsto\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\left({\mathcal{D}_{i}}\right).\,\end{split}

Furthermore, let

(92)

\displaystyle t\left({\mathcal{D}_{i}}\right)=\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{cc}n&\text{if }i=1,2,\\
n-1&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\end{cases}

Then,

(93)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Psi_{(\cdot)}:\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{t\left({\mathcal{D}_{i}}\right)}}\right]}\left({\mathcal{D}_{i}}\right)&\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{m\left({D_{i}}\right)\times m\left({D_{i}}\right)}\\
Z&\mapsto\left[\!\left[{\Psi_{\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{t\left({\mathcal{D}_{i}}\right)}}\right]}\left({\mathcal{D}_{i}}\right)}\left({Z}\right)}\right]\!\right]_{\lambda=\left({t\left({\mathcal{D}_{i}}\right)}\right)}\end{split}

with
\Psi_{(\cdot)}
acting as the isomorphism from
Prop. 6.1
on the corresponding spaces, is a bijection preserving positive semi-definiteness.

Lemma 6.2

(Efficient Bose-symmetric hierarchy with partial trace constraints)
.

Let
\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}\in\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)
and let
C_{i}\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)
denote the
i
-the canonical basis element of this space such that

(94)

\displaystyle\rho_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{1}^{n}}=\sum_{i=1}^{m\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}x_{i}C_{i}\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right).\,

The following problem

(95)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\begin{array}[]{cc}&\displaystyle\displaystyle\Psi\left({SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)}\right)=\max_{\left\{{x_{i}}\right\}_{i=1}^{m\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}}\sum_{i=1}^{m\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}x_{i}\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[{G_{AB}\,C_{i}\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)_{AB}}\right]\\
\\
\text{s.th.}&\sum_{i=1}^{m\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}x_{i}C_{i}\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)_{AB}:=\sum_{i=1}^{m\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}x_{i}\cdot\mathrm{tr}_{\bar{B}_{1}\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{2}^{n}}\left[{C_{i}\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}\right],\\
\\
&\sum_{i=1}^{m\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}x_{i}\cdot\left[\!\left[{\Psi_{\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}\left({C_{i}\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}\right)}\right]\!\right]_{(n)}\succeq 0,\\
\\
&\sum_{i=1}^{m\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}x_{i}\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left[{C_{i}\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}\right]=1,\\
\\
&\sum_{i=1}^{m\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}x_{i}\cdot\left[\!\left[{\Psi_{\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{t\left({\mathcal{D}_{2}}\right)}}\right]}\left({\mathcal{D}_{2}}\right)}\left({\mathrm{tr}_{A_{L}}\left[{C_{i}\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}\right]}\right)}\right]\!\right]_{(n)}\\
\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt=\sum_{i=1}^{m\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}x_{i}\cdot\left[\!\left[{\Psi_{\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{t\left({\mathcal{D}_{2}}\right)}}\right]}\left({\mathcal{D}_{2}}\right)}\left({X_{C_{A_{M}}}\otimes\mathrm{tr}_{A_{L}A_{M}}\left[{C_{i}\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}\right]}\right)}\right]\!\right]_{(n)},\\
\\
&\sum_{i=1}^{m\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}x_{i}\cdot\left[\!\left[{\Psi_{\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{t\left({\mathcal{D}_{3}}\right)}}\right]}\left({\mathcal{D}_{3}}\right)}\left({\mathrm{tr}_{A\left({B_{L}}\right)_{n}\left({\bar{B}}\right)_{n}}\left[{C_{i}\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}\right]}\right)}\right]\!\right]_{(n-1)}\\
\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt=\sum_{i=1}^{m\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}x_{i}\cdot\left[\!\left[{\Psi_{\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{t\left({\mathcal{D}_{3}}\right)}}\right]}\left({\mathcal{D}_{3}}\right)}\left({\mathrm{tr}_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{n}}\left[{C_{i}\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}\right]\otimes Y_{C_{B_{R}}}}\right)}\right]\!\right]_{(n-1)},\end{array}\end{split}

is equivalent as an optimization problem to
SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)
in
Eq. 89
, where we optimize over polynomial many variables subject to polynomial many constraints of polynomial size.

6.3.
Efficiency of the transformation

Consider the program
\Psi\left({SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)}\right)
. By the arguments in the previous section, the size of the SDP is bounded by a polynomial in
n
. However, a direct application of
\Psi
from
Eq. 93
, mapping
SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)
in
Eq. 89
to
\Psi\left({SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)}\right)
in
Eq. 95
, requires exponentially many computations in
n
. Concretely, the quantities in
SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)
are of exponential size in
n
when expressed in the computational basis.

To prove the polynomial-time algorithm stated in
Thm. 2.5
, we provide an efficient procedure for constructing
\Psi\left({SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)}\right)
. The key insight is that, while the objects in
SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)
are exponentially large, they exhibit substantial redundancy. That is, accessing only a polynomial number of entries in
n
suffices to carry out the transformation efficiently.

The structure of this section is as follows. First, in
Sec. 6.3.1
, we establish an efficient method for accessing the unique coefficients of any
Z\in\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\vee^{n}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)}\right)
with respect to the canonical basis of
\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\vee^{n}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)}\right)
. Then, in
Sec. 6.3.2
, we present an efficient procedure for computing
\psi(Z)
for any such
Z
. Finally, in
Sec. 6.3.3
, we conclude with a proof of the overall efficiency of the SDP transformation.

6.3.1.
Efficiently accessing Bose-symmetric operators

At a high level, the task of efficiently determining the unique coefficients of any Bose-symmetric operator amounts to efficiently identifying a representative element to each Bose-symmetric orbit. By
[
Har13
]
,
\left\{{\ket{s_{\vec{t}}}\,:\,\vec{t}\in\mathcal{T}_{(n),\,d_{\mathcal{H}}}}\right\}
with

(96)

\ket{s_{\vec{t}}}:=\sum_{\vec{i}:T(\vec{i})=\vec{t}}\ket{i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}}
or the normalized
\ket{\tilde{s}_{\vec{t}}}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\binom{n}{\vec{t}}}}\ket{s_{\vec{t}}}
,

where
T(\vec{i})
defines the type of
\vec{i}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{n})
is a basis of the symmetric subspace.

Proposition 6.3
.

The set

(97)

\displaystyle\left\{{C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}:=\ket{s_{\vec{t}}}\bra{s_{\vec{t^{\prime}}}}\,:\,\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{(n),\,d_{\mathcal{H}}}}\right\}

is a basis to
\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\vee^{n}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)}\right)
.

Proof.

Since
\text{End}\left({\vee^{n}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)}\right)\simeq\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\vee^{n}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)}\right)
the statement follows. Alternatively, by construction, there are
\left({\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\left({\vee^{n}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)}\right)}\right)^{2}
many nonzero matrices
C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}
with disjoint support.
∎

See
App. E
for an example. Since any Bose-symmetric operator is symmetric, we will provide an alternative characterization of the basis in
Prop. 6.3
from certain linear combinations of basis elements from a basis to
\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\mathcal{H}^{\otimes n}}\right)
. We adopt the notation from
[
Gij09
]
. For
a,b\in\left[{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\right]^{n}
, define
D(a,b)\in\mathbb{N}^{d_{\mathcal{H}}\times d_{\mathcal{H}}}
by

(98)

\displaystyle\left({D(a,b)}\right)_{i,j}=\left\lvert{\left\{{k\,|\,a_{k}=i,b_{k}=j}\right\}}\right\rvert.\,

Note that for any
a,a^{\prime},b,b^{\prime}\in\left[{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\right]^{n}
, we have
D(a,b)=D(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
if and only if
a^{\prime}=\pi(a)
and
b^{\prime}=\pi(b)
for some
\pi\in S_{n}
. Thus, for any such
a,b
the matrix
D_{a,b}
can be seen as an
S_{n}
orbit class function, i.e.

(99)

\displaystyle D(\pi(a),\pi(b))=D(a,b),\,\hskip 28.45274pt\forall\pi\in S_{n}.\,

For the all ones column vector
\vec{1}
, let

(100)

\displaystyle P(n,d_{\mathcal{H}}):=\left\{{D\in\mathbb{N}^{d_{\mathcal{H}}\times d_{\mathcal{H}}}\,|\,\vec{1}^{T}D\vec{1}=n}\right\}

denote the set of such
D
, where the sum over all entries equals to
n
. In other words, any such
D(a,b)
is an encoding of the frequency distribution of the canonical basis to
\mathcal{B}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)
appearing in the pair
a,b
. Furthermore, the set
P(n,d_{\mathcal{H}})
is a collection of representative elements to the orbital decomposition of the computational basis to
\mathcal{B}\left({\mathcal{H}^{\otimes n}}\right)
under the
S_{n}
-action. Note, that
D(a,b)\vec{1}
yields the type of
a
and
D(a,b)^{T}\vec{1}
the type of
b
. From these orbit class functions, we can now construct the canonical basis to
\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\mathcal{H}^{\otimes n}}\right)
by averaging over each orbit. Concretely,

(101)

\displaystyle\left({A_{D}}\right)_{a,b}=\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{cc}1,&\text{if }D(a,b)=D,\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\end{cases}

By
[
Gij09
, Proposition 1]
, the set of
\left\{{A_{D}}\right\}_{D\in P(n,d_{\mathcal{H}})}
is a basis of
\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\mathcal{H}^{\otimes n}}\right)
.
Thus, the following proposition follows naturally.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
a\in\left[{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\right]^{n}
be of type
\vec{t}
and
b\in\left[{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\right]^{n}
be of type
\vec{t^{\prime}}
. Then,

(102)

\displaystyle C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}=\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{cc}1,&\text{if }D(\pi(a),\sigma(b))=D(a,b),\,\forall\pi,\sigma\in S_{n},\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\end{cases}

It is a well-established fact that
P(n,d_{\mathcal{H}})
can be computed efficiently (see e.g.
[
CTV24
]
or
Eq. 301
). Next, we identify a partition of
P(n,d_{\mathcal{H}})
that captures the additional symmetry exhibited by Bose-symmetric operators, in contrast to merely symmetric ones, such that the elements of the resulting set serve as representative elements of the corresponding Bose-symmetric orbits.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
a\in\left[{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\right]^{n}
correspond to a partition
\lambda=\left({\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\ldots,\lambda_{h_{a}}}\right)
of
n
of height
h_{a}\leq d_{\mathcal{H}}
and similarly let
b\in\left[{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\right]^{n}
correspond to a partition
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\ldots,\mu_{h_{b}})
of
n
of height
h_{b}\leq d_{\mathcal{H}}
. Then, for fixed
h_{a},h_{b}

(103)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{Bose}(a,b)=\left\{{D\in P(n,d_{\mathcal{H}})\,|\,D(\pi(a),\sigma(b))=D(a,b),\,\forall\pi,\,\sigma\in S_{n}}\right\}

is a set of cardinality
\mathcal{O}\left({n^{(h_{a}-1)(h_{b}-1)}}\right),\,
i.e. polynomial in
n
, which, given
P(n,d_{\mathcal{H}})
, can be computed efficiently. Furthermore, if
h_{a}=1
or
h_{b}=1
, then
\left\lvert{\mathcal{D}_{Bose}(a,b)}\right\rvert=1
.

Proof.

The problem of determining the cardinality of
\mathcal{D}_{Bose}(a,b)
can be formulated as a contingency table with fixed margins problem (see e.g.
[
LK13
]
). Let
X\in\mathbb{N}^{h_{a}\times h_{b}}
be the contingency matrix such that the entry
X_{k,l}
is the number of systems where
a_{i}=k
and
b_{i}=l
for
i=1,\ldots,n
. Each
X
is subject to fixed margin constraints given by row margins
\sum_{l=1}^{h_{b}}X_{k,l}=\mu_{k}
for each row
k
and column margins
\sum_{k=1}^{h_{a}}X_{k,l}=\lambda_{l}
for each column
l
. The degrees of freedom of each contingency table is thus reduced to
h_{a}h_{b}-\left({h_{a}+h_{b}-1}\right)=\left({h_{a}-1}\right)\left({h_{b}-1}\right).\,
As
n
grows, the problem coincides with determining non-negative integer solutions of dimension
\left({h_{a}-1}\right)\left({h_{b}-1}\right)
in the 2 -way transportation polytope scaled by
\left\{{\mu_{k}}\right\}_{k}
and
\left\{{\lambda_{k}}\right\}_{l}
. Following from Ehrhart’s theorem
[
Ehr62
,
BR15
]
, for fixed dimension, the number of integer points in a scaled polytope grows polynomial in the scale factor. Concretely, let
C(h_{a},h_{b})
characterize the shape of the transportation polytope, then the number of integer points is on the order of
C(h_{a},h_{b})\cdot n^{\left({h_{a}-1}\right)\left({h_{b}-1}\right)}
. If
h_{a}
and
h_{b}
are held constant (do not grow with
n
), then the number of distinct
(h_{a}\times h_{b})
contingency tables with prescribed row sums and column sums is bounded by a polynomial in
n
.
∎

Thus, we obtain an alternative reformulation of the canonical basis for the space of Bose-symmetric operators.

Proposition 6.6
.

Let
a\in\left[{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\right]^{n}
be of type
\vec{t}
,
b\in\left[{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\right]^{n}
be of type
\vec{t^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{D}_{Bose}(a,b)
be defined as in
Lem. 6.5
. Then,

(104)

\displaystyle C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}=\sum_{D\in\mathcal{D}_{Bose}(a,b)}A_{D}.

Lemma 6.7

(Efficient access)
.

Let
Z\in\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\vee^{n}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)}\right)
be a Bose-symmetric operator such that

(105)

\displaystyle Z=\sum_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{(n),\,d_{\mathcal{H}}}}z_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}\,.

Then,
\left\{{z_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}}\right\}_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{(n),\,d_{\mathcal{H}}}}
can be computed in polynomial time w.r.t.
n
.

Proof.

Let

(106)

\displaystyle\begin{split}f_{Z}\,:\,\left\{{0,\ldots,d_{\mathcal{H}}^{n}-1}\right\}\times\left\{{0,\ldots,d_{\mathcal{H}}^{n}-1}\right\}&\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\\
(i,j)&\mapsto Z_{i,j}\end{split}

be a functional oracle that, upon receiving a tuple of indices encoding a matrix entry in the computational basis, returns the corresponding matrix element. Hence, the lemma follows from our ability to query the oracle a number of times polynomial in
n
, sufficient to recover the full collection of coefficients
\left\{{z_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}}\right\}_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{(n),d_{\mathcal{H}}}}
. Based on the results above, we now proceed to describe an algorithmic procedure that accomplishes exactly this.

Algorithm 1

Efficient access Bose-symmetric operators

i\leftarrow 0

\mathcal{K}_{i}\leftarrow P(n,d_{\mathcal{H}})

\mathcal{L}_{i}=\emptyset

while
true
do

i\leftarrow i+1

choose
D\in P(n,d_{\mathcal{H}})

a\leftarrow D\vec{1}
,
b\leftarrow D^{T}\vec{1}

Compute
\mathcal{D}_{Bose}(a,b)

\mathcal{K}_{i+1}=K_{i}\backslash\mathcal{D}_{Bose}(a,b)

\mathcal{L}_{i+1}=\mathcal{L}_{i}\cup f_{Z}(D\vec{1},D^{T}\vec{1})

if

\left\lvert{\mathcal{L}_{i+1}}\right\rvert=\left\lvert{\left\{{z_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}}\right\}_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{(n),d_{\mathcal{H}}}}}\right\rvert

then

Return

\mathcal{L}_{i+1}

break

end

if

end

while

By
Lem. 6.5
, and noting that all relevant sets are of size polynomial in
n
, each step of the algorithm can be executed efficiently. Moreover, since the algorithm performs
\left\lvert{\left\{{z_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}}\right\}_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{(n),d_{\mathcal{H}}}}}\right\rvert=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\left({\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\vee^{n}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)}\right)}\right)
iterations—which is polynomial in
n
—it follows that the coefficients of any Bose-symmetric operator with respect to the canonical basis of
\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\vee^{n}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)}\right)
can be computed efficiently.
∎

6.3.2.
Efficiently transforming Bose-symmetric operators

By linearity, the transformation of any Bose-symmetric operator follows from a transformation of the basis to
\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\vee^{n}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)}\right)
. First we show, that for any
i\in\left[{3}\right]
, given complex coefficients
\left\{{z_{j}}\right\}_{j=1}^{m\left({\mathcal{D}_{i}}\right)}
to a matrix
Z
in terms of the canonical basis to
\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\vee^{n}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)}\right)
, the block

(107)

\displaystyle\left[\!\left[{\Psi_{\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{t\left({\mathcal{D}_{i}}\right)}}\right]}\left({\mathcal{D}_{i}}\right)}\left({Z}\right)}\right]\!\right]_{\lambda=\left({t\left({\mathcal{D}_{i}}\right)}\right)}

can be computed in poly
(n)
time. The canonical basis to
\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{t\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}}\right]}\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)
an be written as

(108)

\displaystyle\left\{{\ket{i}\bra{j}_{A}\otimes\ket{k}\bra{l}_{\bar{A}}\otimes C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}}\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\in\left[{\left\lvert{A}\right\rvert}\right],\,\\
k,l\in\left[{\left\lvert{\bar{A}}\right\rvert}\right],\,\\
\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{(n),\,\left\lvert{BC\bar{B}\bar{C}}\right\rvert}\end{subarray}}

and similarly for
\mathcal{D}_{2}
and
\mathcal{D}_{3}
. Since we assume the dimension of the Hilbert spaces to be fixed, we simplify the notation and restrict to
\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\vee^{n}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)}\right)
. Any
Z\in\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\vee^{n}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)}\right)
can be written in terms of the proposed
\left\{{C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}}\right\}_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{(n),\,d_{\mathcal{H}}}}
basis. By
Prop. 6.1
,
\left[\!\left[{\psi(Z)}\right]\!\right]_{(n)}=U_{(n)}^{T}ZU_{(n)}
, where
U_{(n)}
is the matrix with columns given by polytabloids

(109)

\displaystyle u_{\tau}=\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\sim\tau}\bigotimes_{y\in Y((n))}e_{\tau^{\prime}(y)}.\,

with
\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{(n),\,d_{\mathcal{H}}}
and a standard basis
\left\{{e_{i}}\right\}_{i=1}^{\left\lvert{\mathcal{H}}\right\rvert}
of
\mathcal{H}
. By linearity, we have

(110)

\displaystyle U_{(n)}^{T}ZU_{(n)}=\sum_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{(n),\,d_{\mathcal{H}}}}z_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}U_{(n)}^{T}C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}U_{(n)}.\,

With the bounds on
\mathcal{T}_{(n),\,d_{\mathcal{H}}}
and the definition of
U_{(n)}
, given
\left\{{z_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}}\right\}_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{(n),\,d_{\mathcal{H}}}}
an efficient computation of
\left[\!\left[{\psi(Z)}\right]\!\right]_{(n)}
follows from an efficient computation of
u_{\tau}C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}u_{\gamma}
for any
\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}},\tau,\gamma\in\mathcal{T}_{(n),\,d_{\mathcal{H}}}
.

Proposition 6.8

(
[
Har13
]
)
.

For any
k,N\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\text{Par}\left({k,n}\right)
denote the set of partitions of
n
of at most size
k
. Then,

(111)

\displaystyle\left\lvert{\text{Par}\left({k,n}\right)}\right\rvert=\binom{k+n-1}{n}\leq\left({n+1}\right)^{k},\,\quad\left\lvert{T^{-1}(\vec{t})}\right\rvert=\binom{n}{\vec{t}}:=\frac{n!}{\prod_{i=1}^{k}(t_{i}!)},\,

where
T^{-1}(\vec{t})
is the set of strings of type
\vec{t}
.

Definition 6.9
.

Let
\vec{w}\in
be of type
\vec{t}
. We denote by
\vec{t}-T(w_{i})
the type obtained from
\vec{t}
by erasing the basis element
w_{i}
, i.e.
T(w_{i})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
defined by

(112)

\displaystyle T(w_{i})_{j}=\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{cc}1&\text{if }w_{i}=j,\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\end{cases}

Lemma 6.10
.

Let
\tau,\gamma
be two semi-standard labellings of
Y\left({(n)}\right)
. Given complex numbers
\left\{{z_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}}\right\}_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{(n),\,d_{\mathcal{H}}}}
, then

(113)

\displaystyle\sum_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{d_{\mathcal{H}},n}}z_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}u_{\tau}^{T}C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}u_{\gamma}

can be computed efficiently.

Proof of
Lem. 6.10
.

The proof is an adaptation of
[
Pol20
]
to Bose-symmetric operators with explicit closed-form expressions. Consider
W_{\mathcal{H}}=\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}
with canonical basis
\mathcal{W}:=\left\{{a_{x,y}\,:\,x,y\in\left[{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\right]}\right\}
and, with respect to its natural inner product, its dual space
W^{*}_{\mathcal{H}}=\mathcal{H}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{*}
with dual basis
\mathcal{W}^{*}:=\left\{{a^{*}_{x,y}\,:\,x,y\in\left[{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\right]}\right\}
, i.e.
a^{*}_{x,y}a_{w,z}=\delta_{x,w}\delta_{y,z}.\,
Furthermore, consider the coordinate ring
\mathcal{O}_{n}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)
on
\mathcal{H}
. Let
\mu\left({\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}\right)\in\mathcal{O}_{n}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)\times\mathcal{O}_{n}\left({\mathcal{H}}\right)
be a class function for types
\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}
, i.e. for any
\vec{w},\vec{v}
of type
\vec{t}
and any
\vec{z},\vec{m}
of type
\vec{t^{\prime}}
and for any
\pi,\sigma\in S_{n}
, we have
\mu\left({w,z}\right)=\mu\left({v,m}\right)\,\text{and}\,\mu\left({\pi\left({\vec{w}}\right),\sigma\left({\vec{z}}\right)}\right)=\mu\left({\vec{w},\vec{z}}\right).\,
Furthermore,

(114)

\displaystyle g:=u_{\tau}\otimes u_{\gamma}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau^{\prime}\sim\tau,\,\\
\gamma^{\prime}\sim\gamma\end{subarray}}\bigotimes_{y\in Y\left({(n)}\right)}F_{\tau^{\prime}(y),\gamma^{\prime}(y)}

where
F
is a matrix encoding of
\mathcal{W}^{*}
and
\tau
is row-equivalent to
\tau^{\prime}
and similarly for
\gamma
. Then,

(115)

\displaystyle\begin{split}u_{\tau}^{T}C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}u_{\gamma}&=\left({u_{\tau}\otimes u_{\gamma}}\right)C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}=g\left({C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}}\right)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau^{\prime}\sim\tau,\,\\
\gamma^{\prime}\sim\gamma\end{subarray}}\bigotimes_{y\in Y\left({(n)}\right)}F_{\tau^{\prime}(y),\gamma^{\prime}(y)}\left({\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{w}:T\left({\vec{w}}\right)=\vec{t},\,\\
\vec{z}:T\left({\vec{z}}\right)=\vec{t^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}a_{w_{i},z_{i}}}\right)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{w}:T\left({\vec{w}}\right)=\vec{t},\,\\
\vec{z}:T\left({\vec{z}}\right)=\vec{t^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau^{\prime}\sim\tau,\,\\
\gamma^{\prime}\sim\gamma\end{subarray}}\prod_{y\in Y\left({(n)}\right)}\underbrace{\left({a^{*}_{\tau^{\prime}(y),\gamma^{\prime}(y)}a_{w_{y},z_{y}}}\right)}_{=\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{cc}1,&\text{if }\tau^{\prime}(y)=w_{y}\,\land\,\gamma^{\prime}(y)=z_{y},\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\end{cases}}\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{w}:T\left({\vec{w}}\right)=\vec{t},\,\\
\vec{z}:T\left({\vec{z}}\right)=\vec{t^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau^{\prime}\sim\tau,\,\\
\gamma^{\prime}\sim\gamma\end{subarray}}\delta_{\vec{t},\tau}\delta_{\vec{t^{\prime}},\gamma}\prod_{y\in Y\left({(n)}\right)}\delta_{\tau^{\prime}\left({y}\right),w_{y}}\delta_{\gamma^{\prime}(y),z_{y}}\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{w}:T\left({\vec{w}}\right)=\vec{t},\,\\
\vec{z}:T\left({\vec{z}}\right)=\vec{t^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\delta_{\vec{t},\tau}\delta_{\vec{t^{\prime}},\gamma}=\binom{n}{\vec{t}}\cdot\binom{n}{\vec{t^{\prime}}}\delta_{\vec{t},\tau}\delta_{\vec{t^{\prime}},\gamma},\,\end{split}

where with the connection between types and semistandard labellings of
Y((n))
, i.e.

(116)

\displaystyle\delta_{\vec{t},\tau}=\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{cc}1,&\text{if }\tau\text{ of type }\vec{t},\\
0,&\text{otherwis,e}\end{array}\end{cases}

the expression

\delta_{\tau^{\prime}\left({y}\right),w_{y}}\delta_{\gamma^{\prime}(y),z_{y}}
implies
\delta_{\vec{t},\tau}\delta_{\vec{t^{\prime}},\gamma}
,
\forall y\in Y((n))
. Thus, we have

(117)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{(n),\,d_{\mathcal{H}}}}u_{\tau}^{T}C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}u_{\gamma}\mu\left({\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}\right)&=\sum_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{(n),\,d_{\mathcal{H}}}}g\left({C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}}\right)\mu\left({\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}\right)\\
&=\sum_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{(n),\,d_{\mathcal{H}}}}\binom{n}{\vec{t}}\cdot\binom{n}{\vec{t^{\prime}}}\delta_{\vec{t},\tau}\delta_{\vec{t^{\prime}},\gamma}\mu\left({\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}\right)=\binom{n}{\tau}\cdot\binom{n}{\gamma}\mu\left({\tau,\mu}\right)\end{split}

which, assuming that the multinomial coefficients can be computed efficiently and given
\mu\left({\tau,\mu}\right)
, can be computed efficiently. Thus, replacing these with the corresponding
z_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}
concludes the proof.
∎

6.3.3.
Efficient construction of
\Psi\left({SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)}\right)

Lemma 6.11
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
the transformation
\Psi
mapping
SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)
in
Eq. 89
to
\Psi\left({SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)}\right)
in

can be done in poly
\left({n}\right)
time for systems
A,B
of fixed dimension.

As a direct corollary, we obtain the following result in the fixed non-local game setting.

Corollary 6.12
.

The SDP hierarchy to
Thm. 2.5
(see
Sec. 5.6
) is computable in poly
(n)
time.

Remark 6.13
.

The Bose-symmetric de Finetti theorem in
Lem. 5.7
certifies the approximation error between
SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)
and
cSEP(G)
for any
n
. Since
\Psi
is a
*
-isomorphism,
\Psi\left({SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)}\right)=SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)
, and thus, the rate of convergence translates to
\Psi\left({SDP_{n}^{Bose}(G)}\right)
.

implies that

Since

(119)

\displaystyle C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}\approx\sum_{\vec{i}\,:\,T\left({i}\right)=\vec{t}}\sum_{\vec{j}\,:\,T\left({j}\right)=\vec{t^{\prime}}}\ket{\vec{i}}\bra{\vec{j}},\,

by construction,
C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}
has
\left\lvert{T^{-1}\left({\vec{t}}\right)}\right\rvert\cdot\left\lvert{T^{-1}\left({\vec{t^{\prime}}}\right)}\right\rvert
many non-zero entries and
\left\lvert{T^{-1}\left({\vec{t}}\right)\,\cap\,T^{-1}\left({\vec{t^{\prime}}}\right)}\right\rvert
many non-zero diagonal entries. Clearly, if
\vec{t}\neq\vec{t^{\prime}}
then
T^{-1}\left({\vec{t}}\right)\,\cap\,T^{-1}\left({\vec{t^{\prime}}}\right)=\emptyset
. Thus, since the
S_{n}
-action preserves the type,
C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}
has non-zero diagonal elements, if and only if
\vec{t}=\vec{t^{\prime}}
. But then,
T^{-1}\left({\vec{t}}\right)=T^{-1}\left({\vec{t^{\prime}}}\right)
and
T^{-1}\left({\vec{t}}\right)\,\cap\,T^{-1}\left({\vec{t^{\prime}}}\right)=T^{-1}\left({\vec{t}}\right)
yielding

(120)

\displaystyle\left\lvert{T^{-1}\left({\vec{t}}\right)\,\cap\,T^{-1}\left({\vec{t^{\prime}}}\right)}\right\rvert=\left\lvert{T^{-1}(\vec{t})}\right\rvert=\binom{n}{\vec{t}}.\,

Due to the chosen normalization, the non-zero entries are ones, thus

(121)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathrm{tr}\left[{C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}}\right]=\begin{cases}\binom{n}{\vec{t}}&\text{if }\vec{t}=\vec{t^{\prime}},\\
0&\text{else.}\end{cases}\end{split}

Alternatively, due to linearity of the trace

(122)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathrm{tr}\left[{C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}}\right]&=\mathrm{tr}\left[{\ket{s_{\vec{t}}}\bra{s_{\vec{t^{\prime}}}}}\right]=\sum_{\vec{a}:T\left({\vec{a}}\right)=\vec{t}}\sum_{\vec{b}:T\left({\vec{b}}\right)=\vec{t^{\prime}}}\underbrace{\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{tr}\left[{\ket{a_{i}}\bra{b_{i}}}\right]}_{=\delta_{\vec{a},\vec{b}}\,\Rightarrow\,\vec{t}=\vec{t^{\prime}}}\\
&=\underbrace{\sum_{\vec{a}:T\left({\vec{a}}\right)=\vec{t}}\sum_{\vec{b}:T\left({\vec{b}}\right)=\vec{t^{\prime}}}\delta_{\vec{a},\vec{b}}}_{\displaystyle\sum_{\vec{a}:T\left({\vec{a}}\right)=\vec{t}}1=\binom{n}{\vec{t}}}=\begin{cases}\binom{n}{\vec{t}}&\text{if }\vec{t}=\vec{t^{\prime}},\\
0&\text{else.}\end{cases}\end{split}

Thus, (
2
) is efficiently computable.

We denote types
\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}

&=\sum_{\vec{a}:T\left({\vec{a}}\right)=\vec{t}}\sum_{\vec{b}:T\left({\vec{b}}\right)=\vec{t^{\prime}}}\underbrace{\left({\prod_{i=2}^{n}\mathrm{tr}\left[{\ket{a_{i}}\bra{b_{i}}}\right]}\right)}_{\delta_{\vec{a}_{2}^{n},\vec{b}_{2}^{n}}\,\Rightarrow\,\vec{t}_{red}=\vec{t^{\prime}}_{red}}\ket{a_{1}}\bra{b_{1}}=\sum_{\vec{a}_{2}^{n}:T\left({\vec{a}_{2}^{n}}\right)=\vec{t}_{red}}\ket{a_{1}}\bra{b_{1}}=\binom{n-1}{\vec{t}_{red}}\ket{a_{1}}\bra{b_{1}},\,\end{split}

where we used the fact that

(125)

0&\text{otherwise,}\end{array}\end{cases}

(130)

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{w}\,:\,T\left({\vec{w}}\right)=\vec{t},\,\\

such that

. To clarify,

which implies that the coordinates of

(138)

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{n}}\left[{\ket{i}\bra{j}_{A}\otimes C_{\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}}}^{\vee^{n}}}\right]\otimes Y_{C_{B_{R}}}

over
\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{D}_{3}}
can be computed in poly
(n)
time. Thus,
\mathrm{tr}_{A(B_{L})_{n}\bar{B}_{n}}\left[{C_{i}\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}\right]
and
\mathrm{tr}_{A\left({B\bar{B}}\right)_{n}}\left[{C_{i}\left({\mathcal{D}_{1}}\right)}\right]\otimes Y_{C_{B_{R}}}
can be efficiently written in terms of
\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{D}_{3}}
. We proved
b)
which with
Lem. 6.10
imply that (
6
) and (
7
) are computable in poly
(n)
time.
∎

7.
Approximating quantum non-local games via SDP symmetry reductions

Analogous to the derivation above, we now turn our attention to decomposing

\mathcal{H}^{\otimes n}
as a direct sum of more intricate Weyl modules (see
Prop. C.8
), which is then followed by a further decomposition into a direct sum of Specht modules. The resulting polynomial time algorithm based on SDP symmetry reductions, however, has been extensively studied and appears in various works (e.g.
[
CTV24
,
LPS16
,
FST22
,
Gij09
,
Val09
]
) in the context of different applications. The application to non-local games is a novel contribution. In contrast to the Bose-symmetry-based algorithm of the previous section, the symmetry variant does not require a purification step and therefore outperforms the former when the local dimensions – i.e.,
\left\lvert{A}\right\rvert,\left\lvert{Q}\right\rvert
and
\left\lvert{T}\right\rvert
– are large.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
\lambda\vdash_{d_{\mathcal{H}}}n
be any partition of
n
into at most
d_{\mathcal{H}}
parts. Then,

(139)

\displaystyle\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\mathcal{H}^{\otimes n}}\right)\simeq\bigoplus_{\lambda\vdash_{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\,n}\mathbb{C}^{m_{\lambda}\times m_{\lambda}}

with
m_{\lambda}=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\left({\mathbb{S}_{\lambda}\mathcal{H}}\right)
.

For a canonical basis
\left\{{e_{i}}\right\}_{i=1}^{d_{\mathcal{H}}}
of
\mathcal{H}
define the polytabloid

(140)

\displaystyle u_{\tau}=\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\sim\tau}\sum_{c\in C_{\lambda}}\text{sgn}(c)\bigotimes_{y\in Y(\lambda)}e_{\tau^{\prime}(c(y))}

where
C_{\lambda}
denotes the subgroup of column stabilizers and any
\tau^{\prime}\sim\tau
are related via row stabilizers.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
\lambda\vdash_{d_{\mathcal{H}}}n
be any partition of
n
into at most
d_{\mathcal{H}}
parts. Furthermore, let
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,d_{\mathcal{H}}}
be the set of semistandard Young tableaux with entries from
\left[{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\right]
to Young diagram
Y(\lambda)
and to each partition
\lambda
encoding an isotypical component. Then, there is a representative matrix

(141)

\displaystyle U_{\lambda}=\left[{u_{\tau}\,:\,\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,d_{\mathcal{H}}}}\right]\,\in\,\mathbb{R}^{d^{n}\times m_{\lambda}}

with (polytabloid)
u_{\tau}
be the image of a primitive idempotent from
\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]
in Specht module
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}
as column vector, such that for any
\gamma\in\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,d}
there exists a
\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]
-isomorphism mapping
u_{\tau}
to
u_{\gamma}
. Then, the map

(142)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\psi\,:\,\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\mathcal{H}^{\otimes n}}\right)&\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\lambda\vdash_{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\,n}\mathbb{C}^{m_{\lambda}\times m_{\lambda}}\\
Z&\mapsto\bigoplus_{\lambda\vdash_{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\,n}\left({\left\langle Zu_{\tau},u_{\gamma}\right\rangle_{S_{n}}}\right)_{\tau,\gamma\in\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,_{\mathcal{H}}}}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\vdash_{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\,n}U_{\lambda}^{T}ZU_{\lambda}\,\end{split}

is a bijection preserving positive semidefiniteness.

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a Hilbert space of finite dimension
d_{\mathcal{A}}
. Then, in accordance with
Prop. 7.2
the map

(143)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\psi\,:\,\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\otimes n}}\right)&\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\lambda\vdash_{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\,n}\mathbb{C}^{m_{\lambda}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}^{n})\times m_{\lambda}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}^{n})}\\
Z&\mapsto\bigoplus_{\lambda\vdash_{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\,n}\left({\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}}\otimes U_{\lambda}}\right)^{T}Z\left({\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}}\otimes U_{\lambda}}\right)\end{split}

is a bijection preserving positive semidefiniteness.

See
for a proof with additional quantum side information.

Proposition 7.4
.

For
\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{n}}\right)
in
Prop. 7.3
, there exist the following bounds appearing in the
*
-isomorphism

(144)

\displaystyle m_{\lambda}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}^{n})\leq d_{\mathcal{A}}(n+1)^{\frac{d_{\mathcal{H}}\left({d_{\mathcal{H}}-1}\right)}{2}},\,\forall\lambda\in\text{Par}\,(d_{\mathcal{H}},n),\,

such that

(145)

\displaystyle\begin{split}m\left({\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}^{n}}\right)&:=\dim\left[\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{H}^{n}}\right)\right]\leq d_{\mathcal{A}}^{2}(n+1)^{d_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}\end{split}

Proof.

The direct sum over all partitions
\lambda
of
n
into at most
d_{\mathcal{H}}
parts contains

(146)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\lvert{\text{Par}\,(d_{\mathcal{H}},n)}\right\rvert\leq(n+1)^{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\end{split}

summands. Each summand corresponds to a full matrix algebra
\mathbb{C}^{m_{\lambda}(A,\mathcal{H}^{n})\times m_{\lambda}(A,\mathcal{H}^{n})}
where

(147)

\displaystyle m_{\lambda}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}^{n})=d_{\mathcal{A}}\left\lvert{\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,d_{\mathcal{H}}}}\right\rvert\leq d_{\mathcal{A}}(n+1)^{\frac{d_{\mathcal{H}}\left({d_{\mathcal{H}}-1}\right)}{2}}.\,

Thus,

(148)

\displaystyle\begin{split}m\left({\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}^{n}}\right)&=\left\lvert{\bigoplus_{\lambda\vdash_{d_{\mathcal{H}}}n}\mathbb{C}^{m_{\lambda(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}^{n})}\times m_{\lambda(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}^{n})}}}\right\rvert=\sum_{\lambda\vdash_{d_{\mathcal{H}}}n}\left\lvert{\mathbb{C}^{m_{\lambda(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}^{n})}\times m_{\lambda(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}^{n})}}}\right\rvert\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\autoref{eqn:first_bounds}}}{{\leq}}(n+1)^{d_{\mathcal{H}}}\left\lvert{\mathbb{C}^{m_{\lambda(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}^{n})}\times m_{\lambda(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}^{n})}}}\right\rvert\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\autoref{eqn:first_bounds_2}}}{{\leq}}(n+1)^{d_{\mathcal{H}}}d_{\mathcal{A}}^{2}\left[{(n+1)^{\frac{d_{\mathcal{H}}\left({d_{\mathcal{H}}-1}\right)}{2}}}\right]^{2}=d_{\mathcal{A}}^{2}(n+1)^{d_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}.\,\end{split}

∎

Recall the SDP hierarchy in
Sec. 3
approximating fixed size free non-local games from above. We begin by demonstrating that the analysis can be restricted to the corresponding invariant algebras. Specifically, the following lemma establishes that the
S_{n}
-symmetry is preserved under the given constraints. Subsequently, we apply
Prop. 7.3
to obtain a direct sum decomposition into full matrix algebras.

Lemma 7.5

(Sufficiency in restricting optimization of
SDP_{n}(T,V,\pi)
to a dimension bounded invariant algebra)
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
. If

(149)

\displaystyle\rho_{(A_{1}Q_{1}T)(A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T})_{1}^{n}}\in\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({(A_{1}Q_{1}T)\otimes(A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T})_{1}^{n}}\right),\,

then,

(150)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathrm{tr}_{A_{1}}\left[\rho_{(A_{1}Q_{1}T)(A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T})_{1}^{n}}\right]\in\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({(Q_{1}T)\otimes(A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T})_{1}^{n}}\right),\,\\
\sum_{q_{1}}\pi_{1}(q_{1})\ket{q_{1}}\bra{q_{1}}_{Q_{1}}\otimes\mathrm{tr}_{(A_{1}Q_{1})}\left[\rho_{(A_{1}Q_{1}T)(A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T})_{1}^{n}}\right]&\in\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({(Q_{1}T)\otimes(A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T})_{1}^{n}}\right)\end{split}

together with

(151)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathrm{tr}_{(A_{2})_{1}}\left[\rho_{(A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T})_{1}^{n}}\right]\in\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n-1}}\right]\,}\left({(A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T})_{2}^{n}\otimes(Q_{2}\hat{T})_{1}}\right),\,\\
&\left(\sum_{q_{2}}\pi_{2}(q_{2})\ket{q_{2}}\bra{q_{2}}_{Q_{2}}\otimes\frac{\mathcal{I}_{T}}{\lvert T\rvert}\right)\otimes\mathrm{tr}_{(A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T})_{1}}\left[\rho_{(A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T})_{2}^{n}}\right]\in\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n-1}}\right]\,}\left({(A_{1}Q_{1}T)\otimes(A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T})_{2}^{n}\otimes(Q_{2}\hat{T})_{1}}\right).\,\end{split}

Furthermore,

(152)

\displaystyle\dim\left({\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({(A_{1}Q_{1}T)\otimes(A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T})_{1}^{n}}\right)}\right)\leq\left\lvert{AQT}\right\rvert^{2}(n+1)^{\left\lvert{AQT}\right\rvert^{2}}.\,

Proof.

See
App. D
.
∎

Remark 7.6
.

It is trivial to see, that the constraints

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathrm{tr}\left[{\rho_{(A_{1}Q_{1}T)(A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T})_{1}^{n}}}\right]=1,&&\rho_{(A_{1}Q_{1}T)(A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T})_{1}^{n}}\succcurlyeq 0,\end{array}

can be formulated in terms of a basis to
\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{n}}\right]}\left({(A_{1}Q_{1}T)\otimes(A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T})_{1}^{n}}\right)
.

Remark 7.7
.

Note that it does not matter which of the
(A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T})_{1}^{n}
systems is getting traced out and that the ordering in the tensor product is also irrelevant. Thus,
Prop. 7.3
can be applied to the spaces in
Lem. 7.5
.

7.1.
The block decomposition

We prove
Thm. 2.6
in two steps. First, we demonstrate that determining the optimal winning probability of a fixed-size free two-player non-local game up to an additive error can be formulated as a collection of semidefinite programs with sizes specified in
Thm. 2.6
. Then, we establish that this formulation can be constructed efficiently.

Lemma 7.8
.

There exists an SPD hierarchy indexed in
n
approximating the value of a two-player free non-local game with
\left\lvert{A}\right\rvert
answers
\left\lvert{Q}\right\rvert
questions and quantum assistance of size
\left\lvert{T}\right\rvert
up to an additive error with
\text{poly}(n)
-many variables,
\text{poly}(n)
-many constraints and matrices of at most
\text{poly}(n)
-size as given in
Thm. 2.6
.

Proof.

As in
[
CTV24
]
, we introduce a convenient notation avoiding redundancies between conceptually related concepts. Firstly, let

(154)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}:=\left\{{A_{1}Q_{1}T\otimes\left({A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T}}\right)_{1}^{n},\,\,Q_{1}T\otimes\left({A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T}}\right)_{1}^{n},\,\,(Q_{2}\hat{T})_{1}\otimes\left({A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T}}\right)_{2}^{n}}\right\}

be the set collecting the Hilbert spaces of relevance to our setting. Next, define a function

(155)

\displaystyle\begin{split}t\,:\,\mathcal{D}&\,\rightarrow\,\mathbb{N}\\
\mathcal{D}_{k}&\,\mapsto\,\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{cc}n-1&\text{ for }k=3,\\
n&\text{ otherwise. }\end{array}\end{cases}\end{split}

Furthermore, let

(156)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{I}_{k}:=\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{I}_{A_{1}Q_{1}T}&\text{ for }k=1,\\
\mathcal{I}_{Q_{1}T}&\text{ for }k=2,\\
\mathcal{I}_{\left({Q_{2}\hat{T}}\right)_{1}}&\text{ for }k=3\end{array}\end{cases}\end{split}

denote the corresponding identity operator. To avoid notational issues we will denote
\text{End}_{\mathbb{C}\left[{S_{t\left({\mathcal{D}_{k}}\right)}}\right]}(\cdot)
by
\text{End}^{S_{t\left({\mathcal{D}_{k}}\right)}}(\cdot)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Psi_{\left({\cdot}\right)}\,:\;\text{End}^{S_{t\left({\mathcal{D}_{k}}\right)}}\left({\mathcal{D}_{k}}\right)&\,\rightarrow\,\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\text{Par}\left({\left\lvert{A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T}}\right\rvert,t\left({\mathcal{D}_{k}}\right)}\right)}\mathbb{C}^{m_{\lambda}\left({\mathcal{D}_{k}}\right)\times m_{\lambda}\left({\mathcal{D}_{k}}\right)}\\
Z&\,\mapsto\,\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\text{Par}\left({\left\lvert{A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T}}\right\rvert,t\left({\mathcal{D}_{k}}\right)}\right)}\left[\!\left[\Psi_{\text{End}^{S_{t\left({\mathcal{D}_{k}}\right)}}(\mathcal{D}_{k})}(Z)\right]\!\right]_{\lambda}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\text{Par}\left({\left\lvert{A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T}}\right\rvert,t\left({\mathcal{D}_{k}}\right)}\right)}\left({\mathcal{I}_{k}\otimes U^{T}_{\lambda}}\right)Z\left({\mathcal{I}_{k}\otimes U_{\lambda}}\right),\,\end{split}

where

are the corresponding blocks to

and

(158)

\displaystyle\begin{split}m(\mathcal{D}_{k}):=\sum_{\lambda\in\text{Par}\left({\left\lvert{A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T}}\right\rvert,t\left({\mathcal{D}_{k}}\right)}\right)}m^{2}_{\lambda}\left({\mathcal{D}_{k}}\right)=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\left({\text{End}^{S_{t\left({\mathcal{D}_{k}}\right)}}\left({\mathcal{D}_{k}}\right)}\right)\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\autoref{prop:bounding_our_case}}}{{\leq}}\left\lvert{A_{1}Q_{1}T}\right\rvert^{2}(t\left({\mathcal{D}_{k}}\right)+1)^{\left\lvert{A_{2}Q_{2}\hat{T}}\right\rvert^{2}}\end{split}

be a bijection preserving positive semidefiniteness. Correspondingly, let
\mathcal{C}\left({\mathcal{D}_{k}}\right)=\left\{{C_{i}\left({\mathcal{D}_{k}}\right)}\right\}_{i=1}^{m(\mathcal{D}_{k})}
denote the canonical basis
5

See

.
to
